# 17 November 2026 — bug hunt: notification and announcement route bundles

Audited clean published `main` (`0d7d8a3b`) in a separate worktree so the existing uncommitted feature branch remained untouched. Found reproducible Vite build warnings: the announcement and notification page imports advertised as lazy were ineffective because the global shell synchronously imported those same page modules for banners and the bell. The page code therefore shipped inside the initial app bundle.

Moved only the global announcement card/banner and notification menu/read-state dependencies into small shell-facing modules, and made announcement, inbox and settings routes lazy. The role/permission, online, query and routing behavior is unchanged. Added no dependency or migration. Full frontend suite: 867 tests in 129 files pass; coverage is S73.53/B68.06/F64.89/L76.10%, above the configured thresholds. The three affected UI suites pass46/46; TypeScript, ESLint, Prettier and the synthetic public-config production/PWA build pass. Build no longer reports ineffective dynamic imports; the main JS chunk fell from781.08kB/240.26kB gzip to760.12kB/234.51kB gzip, with separate announcement (2.07kB) and notification (20.33kB) route chunks. Initial `npm ci` reported0 dependency vulnerabilities.

The canonical `./portal test` initially stopped safely when another checkout owned the shared lock, then ran and found two stale expertise expectations plus an `oid[]` initializer warning. The SQL assertions now verify the accepted self-declared-expertise rule, and an additive guarded migration explicitly types the cascade walk's empty array. A subsequent complete run passed source/runtime checks, 867 frontend tests, coverage, 100 Edge tests, packaging, mail/proxy guards, startup/persistence and all25 SQL groups (including50 concurrent claims), then exposed duplicate permissive guest-profile policies through `supabase db advisors`. Added a forward migration merging the narrow guest predicates into existing profile/role policies and dropping the three redundant policies; `npm run test:db` now passes all25 groups, schema lint and advisors with “No issues found.” A final complete acceptance retry currently stops safely because another checkout again holds the shared lock; exact-head CI for the prior commit is still running and must be refreshed after this migration. No foreign process was stopped. No production database, hosting, DNS or email was changed.

# 17 November 2026 — clearer workspace, manual planning and account creation

Implemented the latest user revision as one integrated application change. Chats now devote their viewport to the transcript, with optional search/pin/channel details, compact composition, clear sidebar actions and separated lounge controls. Profile links live in the heading; contact/expertise privacy controls become side-by-side desktop tiles and stack on mobile. Support is available inside Knowledge & Help, with existing ticket deep links retained. The bell and inbox have accessible icon/category navigation for messages, courses, tasks, finance and support; labels distinguish the bounded recent preview and currently loaded history. Invoice status/payment events now generate genuine owner notifications with titles and destinations resolved under current invoice permissions.

Administration has distinct course, assignment, account and customer entry points; the offer form opens contextually and the administrative Kanban remains reachable. Authorised staff can manually allocate a whole published job to an active eligible lecturer, with current contract/topic/conflict/capacity and price-version checks. The staff actor is audited rather than impersonating lecturer consent. Course runs retain explicit customer-organisation linkage and the customer's account membership controls its scoped request visibility. Per-lecturer pricing defaults to50percent; an alternative daily mode starts atEUR200. New commissioned quotes use these server-side settings, count intended local teaching dates, reject unavailable/over-budget quotes and stale consent, and preserve all accepted commercial snapshots. Existing legacy offers without private gross remain their exact legacy net amount; no customer gross is invented or exposed to management.

Admins can create actual password accounts without sending real invitations, choose admin/management/senior lecturer/lecturer/company/guest, and edit permitted ranks. Company creation and lecturer-to-company conversion bind an authorised organisation atomically. Account creation reserves a current-authority request before privileged Auth creation, rechecks permission before completing and only rolls back its freshly created identity on failure. Secrets/passwords never enter browser response data or Git. The later explicit guest request restores a minimal own-profile/settings/static-help entrance; participants remain retired and guests fail operational REST/RPC access. Admin peers may now change ranks, while self/owner protection and stricter activation/deletion hierarchy remain.

ICS imports review local files before one atomic owner-only batch. Supported IANA/UTC/floating timestamps, all-day exclusive ends, recurrence windows, exclusions and moved exceptions are covered; recurrence copies are bounded and are not ongoing subscriptions. Duplicate identities skip safely, invalid/conflicting batches roll back, and an unconfirmed HTTP outcome offers an idempotent retry rather than claiming nothing was committed. Unknown time zones and oversized/invalid calendars are explicit errors.

Actual findings corrected during acceptance: service/anonymous pre-request calls evaluated the operational function without execute permission when the role guard used one boolean expression; nested role guards now preserve the original service/anonymous boundary. Company role dialogs unnecessarily mounted lecturer compensation; rendering now follows actual persisted lecturer roles. Restoring the Kanban entry fixed an orphaned functional module. Native dark-mode review found navy text on saturated blue primary buttons; white text restores contrast. The existing Vite ignore glob also ignored the entire real source when a preview lived below an ancestor `.local`; output ignores now resolve beneath the actual application root, and a real nested-root watcher regression proves source changes are observed while private reports remain ignored.

Verification:867 frontend tests pass,331 Node checks pass, typecheck/ESLint/source formatting and export integrity pass; production build and native Chromium PWA manifest/icons/active-service-worker/installability checks pass. Focused actual PostgreSQL:15 commission,2 individual-price/manual-assignment,11 account groups, creation/guest/atomic-company checks,4 calendar-import/invoice groups,27 existing deletion groups and the owned cascade regression pass. All22 account-facade Deno tests pass. Native actual Auth/UI verifies six account types, company binding and conversion, administrator peer rank changes, restricted guest access, manual two-session allocation atEUR400, immutable accepted price after settings change, customer-account request scoping, and ICS preview/save/timezone/reimport at1440/390/320px. These focused results do not certify the complete historical canonical API/E2E/PWA suite or hosted exact-head CI. Visual acceptance passes after three review/fix passes, including mobile category grid44px targets and dark-mode white-on-blue primary labels. Final transcript heights are649/303/463px across the tested desktop/mobile/dark states; no final horizontal overflow or page JavaScript errors. The scoped comparison and remaining verification limits are recorded in root design-qa.md. The integrated source was pushed to main and the persistent preview at5773 was updated without resetting data. A fresh private PostgreSQL archive and verified catalogue preceded restart; its existing project/port overlay, Auth/Storage volumes and password were preserved. All four forward migrations are present. Native admin login and the new calendar/import, administration overview/board entry, account-create dialog, Knowledge & Help Support tab and profile heading action pass there. The repaired nested-source watcher also passed an actual candidate CSS declaration invalidation check in addition to its isolated regression. No production hosting/data or real email was changed.

## 15 November 2026 — independent frontend/API review (before build acceptance)

Read-only review identified issues for the parent implementer to resolve before browser acceptance:

1. Bind claim consent to the exact reviewed job/terms version. Refetched terms must invalidate existing checkbox consent, and job-ID changes must reset mutation result/idempotency state; a prior confirmation must never be reused for another job.
2. Combined date filters must match a session within both bounds. Separate “some date after from” and “some date before to” can falsely match a block whose sessions straddle an empty range.
3. Profile biography validation must align with the database's 4,000-character limit rather than allowing 5,000.
4. Clear private query state on authenticated actor-ID changes, not only explicit sign-out, because shared query keys otherwise risk displaying a previous actor's in-memory results.
5. Complete remaining hard-coded decorative DE/EN text and handle reload/cancellation of unfinished MFA enrollment without trapping a user behind a discarded QR/factor.

These are review findings, not claimed final-state failures or fixes. The parent is integrating corrections; final checks below must record their actual resolution. Official Supabase changelog/CLI/API sources were checked and recorded in `REFERENCE_SOURCES.md`; invitation function CORS was highlighted to the backend agent.

### CI runtime: isolated desktop and mobile browser lanes

The exact PR #42 run `44012150083` showed the `verify` and retained-data selfhost gates failing in the same fictional Auth fixture cleanup: GoTrue returned HTTP 500 while deleting an account; the 699-test coverage job itself passed in 1m38s. Desktop/mobile browser acceptance had planned 160 workflows on one worker. Earlier PR #42 commits now run independent PostgreSQL suites concurrently against disposable migrated database clones and serialize Auth-backed API suites that share one GoTrue service. The following full run got past the Auth failure, then exposed a 4,000-emoji JSON result mismatch: the SQL helpers decoded each stdout chunk independently and could split a UTF-8 character. They now use a shared `StringDecoder` collector with a regression test for split supplementary characters. The large Unicode case also returns a compact server-side base64 equality proof plus character and byte counts, preserving an exact 120/4,000-character assertion without emitting a large Unicode value. Full current-head CI remains required.

To reduce browser wall time without dropping any browser/device cases, PR #42 now runs the complete browser inventory in two independent jobs: one desktop Chromium lane and one mobile Chromium lane. Each lane owns its own isolated Supabase stack, verifies discovery and execution against exactly its assigned project, and keeps the existing built-PWA tests. Local acceptance without a lane selector still runs both projects. This duplicates isolated service startup but allows both complete device inventories to run at once. The final preview gate waits for both matrix legs. Validation before submission: focused browser-evidence tests, syntax/format checks; full acceptance pending on the new PR head.

The first matrix CI run correctly caught stale workflow-policy fixtures that still expected one unsuffixed browser job. Updated them to assert the two exact matrix lanes and continue checking that the browser job runs only for pull requests. This is a test expectation update; all required matrix jobs remain fail-closed in the existing final gate.

The next exact-head run passed those policy fixtures and then caught the acceptance-report artifact test still expecting the old single browser filename. The expectation now includes `${{ matrix.browser-project }}` so each lane uploads distinct evidence; its 17 local workflow-scope tests pass.

The native SQL rerun confirmed the Unicode limits but PostgreSQL wrapped the expected Base64 text, making a string comparison false. The test now compares stored UTF-8 bytes (`bytea`) directly against decoded expected bytes, while retaining the full API response equality and explicit character/byte counts.

## 17 November 2026 — main migration version collision

The latest `main` had two SQL migration files with version `20261117130000`: `company_lecturer_decisions` and `remove_mfa_and_assurance_gates`. Supabase uses the version as the migration primary key, so a clean local replay stopped with SQLSTATE 23505 before the Portal could finish starting. Renamed the latter migration to `20261117130021` (the intervening `30001` and `30002` versions are already used) and updated its SQL regression harness path. Validation: all 850 Vitest tests pass; a fresh isolated local Supabase/Portal startup applied the complete migration set and recorded `20261117130021:remove_mfa_and_assurance_gates`; `/login` returned HTTP 200; and the TypeScript/Vite/PWA production build passed. A full `./portal test` attempt stopped at the pre-existing lint errors in `deploy/hostinger/maintenance/candidate-upgrade-probe.mjs` (`totp` unused and `createHmac` undefined), before its isolated database/browser stages. The isolated verification stack was stopped and its temporary port overlay reverted. No SQL migration body changed.

## 17 November 2026 — local admin credentials and stale bootstrap marker

The local runtime treated any `.local/bootstrap.json` as proof that the current Supabase project had already been seeded. Changing to a fresh local project left that marker behind, so its empty Auth database received no admin account. Startup now checks the active loopback Auth service each time: seed only when it has no accounts, then record the current project ID; existing local accounts and changed passwords remain untouched. For an empty local Auth instance, `local-env.mjs` now assigns the stable fictional test password already given to the user and prints it through `./portal credentials`. The fixed value is consumed only after Supabase status is verified as loopback and is not part of the frontend or hosted configuration.

Three focused Node regression tests cover stale markers with both empty and populated Auth, plus password selection; targeted ESLint and Prettier pass. All 850 Vitest tests and the TypeScript/Vite/PWA build pass. The helper was exercised against the local Auth service: it seeded the empty active project and the admin password grant returned HTTP 200. A full `./portal start` rehearsal did not reach Ready because Supabase CLI reported `Expected local service db is not running` during startup; after that, its API and database were running and the direct bootstrap/login check passed. The full canonical test runner also remains blocked before integration/E2E by the unrelated lint failures recorded above. No production or customer data was touched.
